Preface
This text book, Advanced Mathematics for Secondary Schools is written specifically
for Form Six students in the United Republic of Tanzania. It is written in accordance
with the 2010 Advanced Mathematics Syllabus for Advanced Level Secondary Schools
Education, Form V - VI issued by the then, Ministry of Education and Vocational
Training.
The book consists of eight chapters, namely; Coordinate geometry II, Vectors,
Hyperbolic functions, Statistics, Probability, Complex numbers, Differential equations,
and Numerical methods. Each chapter contains activities, projects, and exercises. The
answers for proofs, show, verifications and some illustrations are not provided. You
are encouraged to do all the activities, exercises, and projects together with any other
assignments that your teacher will provide. Doing so, will enable you to develop the
intended competencies.
Tanzania Institute of Education

Chapter One
Coordinate geometry II
Introduction
Y
The concept of coordinate geometry you learnt in Form Five enabled you to develop
competencies in finding general equations of circles, equations of tangent and normal
L
to a circle, point of intersection of circles, length of tangent from a point to a circle,
N
area of a rectangle, angle between two lines, perpendicular distance of a point from
a line, and equation of an angle bisector. Also, you learnt about locus problems,
O
proving and applying the ratio theorem in solving related problems, proving properties
of parallelogram, and some real life applications. In this chapter, you will learn the
concept of conic sections, types of conic sections, poElar coordinates, and various
applications of conic sections. The competencies developed will help you in solving
S
various problems in real life such as adjusting dish antennas for radio and television
channels, projecting objects, studying solar systems, manufacturing telescopes for
U
studying the universe, ship navigations, focusing light for cooking and heating, among
many other applications.
E

When a cone is cut by a plane parallel to
the generating line, a parabola is formed.
When a plane intersects with the axis of
the cone at an angle less than or greater
than 90°, an ellipse is formed. A hyperbola
O
is formed when a plane parallel to the axis
of a cone intersects with two nappes.
In geometry, the shape of the conic section Y
obtained is considered to be a locus of a
L
point. A locus is a set of all points whose
coordinates are precisely defined by an Figure 1.2: Different geomNetrical properties
of a parabola

O
a conic section is a locus of a point which
moves in such a way that its distance from
a fixed point, called focus, bears a constant E
ratio to its distance from a fixed line, called
S
directrix. The aim of this chapter is to
study the nature and properties of parabola, U
ellipse, and hyperbola.

Properties of conic sections

I

L

N
known as eccentricity.

Chapter Two
DO NOT DUPLICATE
Vectors
Introduction
One of the most important human activities is mobility. A process of moving from one
Y
point to another encompasses displacement, velocity, momentum, acceleration, and
force. In order to describe motion correctly the knowledge of vector quantities is very
L
important. Vectors are referred to as the quantities that contain both magnitude and
direction. In this chapter, you will learn how to represent a vector in thNree-dimensional
space, find a unit vector in a given direction in three-dimensional space, derive ratio
theorem, define and calculate the dot product of vectors, and findO the angle between two
vectors. You will also learn how to find the projection of a vector onto another vector,
find the work done by forces, prove cosine rule using dot prod uct of vectors, define and
calculate cross product of vectors, and use cross produEct of vectors to prove the sine
rule and calculate the area of a triangle and parallelogram. In addition, you will learn
S
how to differentiate and integrate a vector valued function, and use the knowledge of
differentiation and integration to determine velocity and acceleration. The competencies
U
developed will help you in solving real life problems related to projectile motion which
involves projecting objects such as balls in sports and missiles in military activities,
transportation, engineering, navigations, industries, and other activities in everyday
life. E
N

Since integration is the reverse of differentiation, taking the anti-derivatives will bring
back the vector r. Thus, just in the same way as it was observed in Activity 2.12,
integration of a vector does not change the direction of the vector. Integration is done
by integrating the coefficients of the directional vectors.

Chapter Three
DO NOT DUPLICATE
Hyperbolic functions
Introduction
Functions that are associated with the geometry of a hyperbola are called hyperbolic
Y
functions. Hyperbolic functions are defined through algebraic expressions which
involve the exponential functions, ex and e–x. These functions are sometimeLs referred
to as the hyperbolic trigonometric functions as they have similar properties, but
N
not quite the same as the standard trigonometric functions. In this chapter, you will
O
learn how to define hyperbolic cosine, sine, tangent, secant, cosecant, and cotangent;
sketch graphs of hyperbolic cosine and sine functions, and their inverses; convert
hyperbolic functions into logarithmic functions; differentiate hyperbolic functions;
E
define power series of hyperbolic functions; and integrate hyperbolic functions. The
S
competencies developed will help you to solve problems in various fields such as
in architecture, electrical engineering, staUtistical mechanics, modeling population
growth and decay, geometry, map projection, theory of relativity, among many other
applications.
E
N

Since the graph of y = sinh x is symmetrical
about the origin, it is an odd function. The
graph of y = cosh x is symmetrical about
the y-axis, hence it is an even function.

Chapter Four
Statistics
Introduction
Statistics is concerned with scientific methods for collecting, organizing, summarizYing,
presenting, and analysing data for the purpose of drawing valid conclusions and
L
making reasonable decisions based on such analysis. Statistics is classified into two
N
types; descriptive and inferential statistics. Descriptive statistics involves providing
simple summaries about the sample data, while inferential staOtistics is the act of
generalizing the conclusion from the data or sample to a larger population with
calculated degree of certainty. In this chapter, you will learn the measures of central
E
tendency and dispersion. The competencies developed will help you to collect,
S
analyze, summarize, and interpret data in different sectors such as education, health,
business, agriculture, and many other fields. U
Measures of central tendency E
A measure of central tendency is a value that describes a set of data by identifying
N
the central position of a given set of values. The central tendency of a statistical
I
value means a typical value around which all other values tend to concentrate.
L
Each in a different sense tells what value of the data is clustered around.
N
A measure of central tendency indicates the location or the general position
of the distributOion on the x-axis. It is also known as a measure of location or
position. The following activity will help you to learn about the measures of
central tendency.
R
O

Chapter Five
Probability
Introduction Y
In everyday life there are events whose outcomes cannot be determined before they
L
occur, or are non-derterministic. For instance, in any random experiment, there is
N
always uncertainty as to whether or not a particular event will occur. Probability
measures the chance or likelihood of an event to occur. It is abOout prediction of
uncertainties which require the laws of chance. In this chapter, you will learn
the counting techniques for the sample spaces, probability axioms, theorems and
E
properties. You will also learn conditional probability, probability distributions, and
S
probability distributions which are binomial, Poisson, and normal distributions.
The competencies developed will enable you tUo solve real-life problems related to
predicting events to occur such as rainfall, gambling and betting, currency exchange
trading, stock markets, prices of commodities, and many other related applications.
E
Counting techniques N
there are u × u × u ×  × u events in

Chapter Six
Complex numbers
Introduction
A complex number is an element of the number system that contains real numberYs and
a specific element denoted by i, called the imaginary unit. Complex numbers were
L
introduced to provide solutions to polynomial equations which do not have solutions
N
in the real number system. In complex numbers, the solution of a polynomial equation
is known as a root. In this chapter, you will learn the concept of complex numbers and
O
their operations, complex numbers in polar form, De Moivre’s theorem, and Euler’s
formula. The competencies developed will help you in solving real life problems
such as generating algorithms for performing operatioEns like addition, subtraction,
multiplication, and division in digital computer systems. The knowledge of complex
S
numbers is also applicable in many fields such as alternating current circuit analysis,
quantum mechanics, fluid dynamics, vibraUtion analysis, signal processing, among
many other applications.
E

Chapter Seven
Differential equations
Introduction
Y
An equation involving the derivatives of a function of one or more variables is
called a differential equation. Many laws which describe physical phenomLena such
as biological sciences and engineering are expressed as differential equations. It is
N
therefore essential to learn methods leading to their solutions and applications. In
O
this chapter, you will learn about the introduction to differential equations, solution
to differential equations, first order differential equations, second order differential
equations, and applications of differential equations. The competencies developed will
E
help you to apply it in various real life problems such as in determining population
S
increase or decrease, prediction of infectious diseases, cooling of bodies, determining
rates of decay of radioactive particles, anUd many other applications.
Introduction to differentialE equations

N
involving derivatives of one or more function of only one independent variable
dependent variables withI respect to one is called an ordinary differential equation.

Chapter Eight
Numerical methods
Introduction
Y
Numerical methods offer approximate solutions to mathematical problems where,
L
either analytical methods do not exist or the data available do not allow the
application of known analytical methods. In this chapter, you will learn about
N
errors, roots of a function by iterative methods, and numerical integration. The
O
competencies developed will help you to solve problems in the fields of engineering,
medicine, and social sciences. Also, in solving mathematical problems formulated
from real life situations whose solutions can be obtained numerically and in many
E
other applications.
